## `reviews/` â€” convenÃ§Ã£o de nomenclatura e classificaÃ§Ã£o

EvidÃªncia de rodada, mesmo papel que `docs/architecture/history/` tem para decisÃµes de arquitetura: nunca normativo, sÃ³ prova de como uma nota/decisÃ£o foi alcanÃ§ada. TrÃªs geraÃ§Ãµes de conteÃºdo coexistem aqui, nesta ordem cronolÃ³gica:

## 4. Protocolo de debate Claude Ã¢â€ â€ Codex

Aplica-se **obrigatoriamente** a: decisÃƒÂµes de arquitetura, requisitos, modelo de dados, seguranÃƒÂ§a/privacidade, e qualquer entregÃƒÂ¡vel explicitamente submetido ao protocolo (Type 1, difÃƒÂ­cil de reverter Ã¢â‚¬â€ nÃƒÂ­vel 5-6 de `docs/engineering/change-risk-scale.md`). **NÃƒÂ£o ÃƒÂ© obrigatÃƒÂ³rio** para: correÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o mecÃƒÂ¢nica, documentaÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o factual, refactors locais reversÃƒÂ­veis, lint/teste, implementaÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o direta de decisÃƒÂ£o jÃƒÂ¡ aprovada (nÃƒÂ­veis 1-4) Ã¢â‚¬â€ usar a escala de risco para calibrar, nÃƒÂ£o julgamento ad-hoc repetido.

Quando aplicÃƒÂ¡vel: mÃƒÂ­nimo 3 rodadas (proposta Ã¢â€ â€™ crÃƒÂ­tica Ã¢â€ â€™ trÃƒÂ©plica), nota mÃƒÂ­nima 9.0 de ambos antes de considerar concluÃƒÂ­do, sem arredondar (8.99 nÃƒÂ£o vira 9). Protocolo de nota cega: o avaliador que responde depois nÃƒÂ£o vÃƒÂª a nota/parecer do primeiro atÃƒÂ© ambos existirem registrados; desacordo abaixo de 9 reabre rodada em vez de arredondar ou fazer mÃƒÂ©dia.

**Pesquisa externa antes da Rodada 1 (2026-08-30, E-014)**: uma decisÃƒÂ£o nÃƒÂ­vel 5-6 que define um padrÃƒÂ£o que sistemas fora deste projeto jÃƒÂ¡ resolveram de forma estabelecida (RBAC, convite/invite, sessÃƒÂ£o multi-tenant, etc.) exige, na prÃƒÂ³pria proposta Rodada 1, uma declaraÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o explÃƒÂ­cita `SIM`/`SIM PARCIAL`/`NÃƒÆ’O` (nunca omissÃƒÂ£o) e, se `SIM`/`SIM PARCIAL`, um checklist de critÃƒÂ©rios pesados derivado da pesquisa que vira a rÃƒÂ©gua de nota da rodada Ã¢â‚¬â€ critÃƒÂ©rio completo, fluxo de reconciliaÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o se o Codex contestar o checklist, e exemplos: `docs/engineering/research-protocol.md`.

O protocolo ÃƒÂ© dispensÃƒÂ¡vel para uma decisÃƒÂ£o Type 1 quando o responsÃƒÂ¡vel final (`AGENTS.md` Ã‚Â§1) jÃƒÂ¡ decidiu diretamente Ã¢â‚¬â€ mas sÃƒÂ³ sob as trÃƒÂªs condiÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂµes registradas em `docs/engineering/ai-governance.md` Ã‚Â§2 (achado real de `full-audit-round1-governanca-ia`: ADR-0009 dispensou o protocolo sem essa exceÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o estar formalizada). InvocaÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o do Codex: `codex exec --skip-git-repo-check "<prompt>"`, rodar em background. **Nunca usar crases (`` ` ``) dentro de um prompt passado por Bash com aspas duplas** Ã¢â‚¬â€ o shell interpreta como substituiÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o de comando e corrompe a entrada silenciosamente (o processo trava esperando stdin, CPU ~0). Para prompts com crases/markdown, escrever em arquivo e usar `codex exec --skip-git-repo-check - < arquivo.txt`. Se um processo `codex` rodar muito mais que rodadas comparÃƒÂ¡veis com CPU quase zero, estÃƒÂ¡ travado Ã¢â‚¬â€ matar e relanÃƒÂ§ar, nÃƒÂ£o esperar. **NÃƒÂ£o combinar `- < arquivo.txt` com backgrounding (`&`)** Ã¢â‚¬â€ essa combinaÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o jÃƒÂ¡ produziu duas falhas silenciosas (processo travado relendo arquivo grande e bloqueado por polÃƒÂ­tica de sandbox; depois saÃƒÂ­da vazia sem erro) mesmo com prompt correto. Rodar `codex exec --skip-git-repo-check - < arquivo.txt > saida.txt 2>&1` em primeiro plano (sem `&`) ÃƒÂ© o padrÃƒÂ£o confiÃƒÂ¡vel verificado; sÃƒÂ³ background se o prompt for curto o bastante para passar inline sem stdin.

**Regras de shell especÃƒÂ­ficas do ambiente real de trabalho (Windows, terminal com aprovaÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o de comando por chamada)** Ã¢â‚¬â€ achado real de `full-audit-round1-contexto-summary.md` (critÃƒÂ©rio Portabilidade AgnÃƒÂ³stica): as regras acima descrevem semÃƒÂ¢ntica de Bash/backgrounding em termos genÃƒÂ©ricos, mas duas regras adicionais sÃƒÂ³ fazem sentido neste ambiente especÃƒÂ­fico e nÃƒÂ£o devem ser assumidas como universais ao portar o processo para outro agente/SO: (1) **nunca encadear comandos com `&&`** (nem sÃƒÂ³ dois passos triviais como `cd` + outro comando) Ã¢â‚¬â€ cada aÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o ÃƒÂ© uma chamada de shell separada; encadear dispara um prompt de aprovaÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o que um agente em background nÃƒÂ£o consegue responder. (2) **Reafirmar o diretÃƒÂ³rio de trabalho (`cd` isolado, sua prÃƒÂ³pria chamada) antes de qualquer comando sempre que houver uma lacuna** Ã¢â‚¬â€ depois de aguardar notificaÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o, depois de comando longo, no inÃƒÂ­cio de cada retomada Ã¢â‚¬â€ o diretÃƒÂ³rio de trabalho pode resetar para a raiz entre chamadas/turnos; nÃƒÂ£o presumir que persiste. (3) **Ao esperar um run de CI/CD recÃƒÂ©m-disparado, nunca faÃƒÂ§a polling de "existe um run completo"** Ã¢â‚¬â€ um run antigo (ex. um deploy que jÃƒÂ¡ falhou antes) satisfaz essa condiÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o na hora e produz um "concluÃƒÂ­do" falso enquanto o run real ainda nem comeÃƒÂ§ou (achado real, recorrente nesta sessÃƒÂ£o, 2026-08-31). Capture o `databaseId` do run mais recente ANTES do trigger, espere um `databaseId` diferente aparecer, sÃƒÂ³ entÃƒÂ£o faÃƒÂ§a polling desse run especÃƒÂ­fico atÃƒÂ© `completed` Ã¢â‚¬â€ e confira o estado com uma chamada direta de vez em quando em vez de confiar cegamente numa ÃƒÂºnica espera longa em background.

## 7. ConvenÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂµes e invariantes de cÃƒÂ³digo real

Este diretÃƒÂ³rio trata de **como o trabalho de engenharia ÃƒÂ© medido e revisado** (rubrica, critÃƒÂ©rios por eixo, protocolo de debate, achados de auditoria) Ã¢â‚¬â€ nÃƒÂ£o confundir com `docs/architecture/`, que trata do que o sistema Ãƒâ€° (design, modelo de dados, decisÃƒÂµes de arquitetura). ## PrecedÃƒÂªncia de fontes (quando houver divergÃƒÂªncia)

## Escopo da decisÃƒÂ£o

1. **Janela de quarentena/recuperaÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o antes de exclusÃƒÂ£o fÃƒÂ­sica irreversÃƒÂ­vel** Ã¢â‚¬â€ cross-cutting, nÃƒÂ£o
   sÃƒÂ³ para `Organization`/tenant. Hoje `CloseOrganizationService`/W3-07 sÃƒÂ³ tem `Wait(1800s)` entre
   `DELETINGÃ¢â€ â€™QUIESCING` (`infra/state-machines/tenant-purge.asl.json`) Ã¢â‚¬â€ operacional (drenar
   requests em voo antes do fence apertar), nÃƒÂ£o uma janela de arrependimento: nÃƒÂ£o existe aÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o real
   de "cancelar exclusÃƒÂ£o", e `TenantLifecycleStatus` ÃƒÂ© forward-only por design (`ACTIVE` nunca ÃƒÂ©
   reentrada Ã¢â‚¬â€ `tenant-lifecycle-record.ts` linha 27).
2. **7 de 9 `retentionClass` sem purga fÃƒÂ­sica real** (`docs/architecture/privacy-lgpd.md` Ã‚Â§4) Ã¢â‚¬â€
   verificado por leitura de cÃƒÂ³digo, nÃƒÂ£o sÃƒÂ³ do doc: `USER_DOCUMENT` tem `DocumentPurgeWorker`
   (W3-06) e `EXTRACTION_TRANSIENT` tem lifecycle S3 de 24h; `TRANSIENT`/`InvitationTokenPointer`
   jÃƒÂ¡ tem `purgeAfterTtl` fÃƒÂ­sico real (`invitation-token.ts`). `ACCOUNT_ACTIVE`/`CORE_USER_DATA` sÃƒÂ£o
   varridas fisicamente hoje SÃƒâ€œ no caso de fechamento de tenant inteiro (`purgeTenant()`/W3-07) Ã¢â‚¬â€
   **nÃƒÂ£o hÃƒÂ¡ purga por idade dentro de um tenant `ACTIVE`** (ex.: um `DELIVERY_RECORD` individual nÃƒÂ£o
   ÃƒÂ© apagado aos 180 dias enquanto a Organization segue aberta). `LEGAL_EVIDENCE`,
   `SECURITY_AUDIT`, `QUOTA_TELEMETRY` nÃƒÂ£o tÃƒÂªm nenhum worker de purga por idade. Confirmado por
   grep: nenhum cron/scheduler alÃƒÂ©m de `tenant-purge-sweeper` (pÃƒÂ³s-`DELETED`) e o TTL nativo do
   DynamoDB usado sÃƒÂ³ por `InvitationTokenPointer`.

**Fora de escopo desta rodada** (explÃƒÂ­cito, nÃƒÂ£o implÃƒÂ­cito): (a) a duraÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o/janela operacional de
1800s jÃƒÂ¡ `APPROVED` D-066 Rodada H Ã¢â‚¬â€ nÃƒÂ£o redecidida; (b) implementaÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o de todos os 7 workers de
purga por classe Ã¢â‚¬â€ esta rodada decide MECANISMO + PRIORIZAÃƒâ€¡ÃƒÆ’O, nÃƒÂ£o entrega os 7; (c) a futura
feature de armazenamento de arquivos Ã¢â‚¬â€ ainda nÃƒÂ£o escopada, esta rodada sÃƒÂ³ garante que o mecanismo
de quarentena decidido ÃƒÂ© genÃƒÂ©rico o bastante para ela reusar depois, sem desenhar a feature em si.

## Pesquisa externa considerada: SIM

PadrÃƒÂ£o bem estabelecido fora deste projeto Ã¢â‚¬â€ janela de recuperaÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o antes de exclusÃƒÂ£o fÃƒÂ­sica
definitiva ÃƒÂ© uma prÃƒÂ¡tica amplamente convergente em produtos B2B/SaaS:

- **GitHub** (`docs.github.com/en/repositories/creating-and-managing-repositories/restoring-a-deleted-repository`,
  acessado 2026-08-31): repositÃƒÂ³rio deletado ÃƒÂ© restaurÃƒÂ¡vel por **90 dias**; apÃƒÂ³s isso, apagado
  permanentemente, sem bypass self-service (sÃƒÂ³ suporte, caso a caso). RestauraÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o cobre org owners
  para repos da organizaÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o, nÃƒÂ£o sÃƒÂ³ o autor.
- **AWS** (`docs.aws.amazon.com/accounts/latest/reference/manage-acct-closing.html`, acessado
  2026-08-31): conta fechada entra em "post-closure period" de **90 dias**, reabrÃƒÂ­vel nesse prazo;
  recursos remanescentes sÃƒÂ£o apagados automaticamente sÃƒÂ³ depois de decorridos os 90 dias.
- **Google Workspace** (`workspace.google.com/learn-more/security/security-whitepaper/data-recovery`,
  acessado 2026-08-31): usuÃƒÂ¡rio deletado ÃƒÂ© restaurÃƒÂ¡vel por administrador por **20 dias**; dados de
  Drive/Gmail especificamente por atÃƒÂ© 25 dias. Janela mais curta que GitHub/AWS, mesma famÃƒÂ­lia de
  padrÃƒÂ£o (aÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o administrativa explÃƒÂ­cita de restauraÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o dentro de um prazo fixo, depois disso
  irrecuperÃƒÂ¡vel mesmo via suporte).
- **Slack** (`slack.com/help/articles/203457187-Customize-data-retention-in-Slack`, acessado
  2026-08-31): arquivos sem compartilhamento tÃƒÂªm grace period de **30 dias** antes de apagados
  definitivamente Ã¢â‚¬â€ dado adicional convergente, nÃƒÂ£o sobre exclusÃƒÂ£o de workspace inteiro.

ConvergÃƒÂªncia real: 3 de 4 fontes (GitHub, AWS, e o precedente-irmÃƒÂ£o Slack-arquivo) usam algo entre
30-90 dias; Google Workspace ÃƒÂ© o outlier mais curto (20-25 dias), mas ainda na mesma ordem de
grandeza (dias-a-semanas, nunca minutos/horas, nunca ilimitado). Nenhuma fonte usa quarentena
medida em segundos/minutos para dado genuinamente persistido (todas usam o Wait(1800s) atual sÃƒÂ³
para o problema operacional distinto de drenar requests em voo).

## Checklist de critÃƒÂ©rios de nota (derivado da pesquisa)

```text
1. (peso 25%) DuraÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o da quarentena escolhida cita a pesquisa real (nÃƒÂ£o um nÃƒÂºmero arbitrÃƒÂ¡rio) e cai
   dentro ou justifica desvio da faixa convergente (20-90 dias) Ã¢â‚¬â€ nÃƒÂ£o pode ser um valor sem
   fundamento externo nem interno.
2. (peso 20%) Mecanismo ÃƒÂ© genuinamente cross-cutting (nomeado como polÃƒÂ­tica reusÃƒÂ¡vel por qualquer
   feature futura de "dado persistido + exclusÃƒÂ£o irreversÃƒÂ­vel", nÃƒÂ£o hardcoded sÃƒÂ³ para
   Organization/W3-07) sem exigir reescrever o pipeline atual de W3-07 do zero.
3. (peso 20%) AÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o real de "cancelar exclusÃƒÂ£o" ÃƒÂ© decidida explicitamente (existe ou nÃƒÂ£o, e por
   quÃƒÂª) Ã¢â‚¬â€ nÃƒÂ£o pode ficar ambÃƒÂ­guo se um OWNER que erra tem como voltar atrÃƒÂ¡s.
4. (peso 15%) Prioridade das 7 classes LGPD ÃƒÂ© justificada por sensibilidade/exposiÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o real
   (dado pessoal identificÃƒÂ¡vel vs. telemetria agregada) e por sinal jÃƒÂ¡ registrado no prÃƒÂ³prio
   `privacy-lgpd.md` (ex. `LEGAL_EVIDENCE` tem trava jurÃƒÂ­dica adicional, nÃƒÂ£o ÃƒÂ© "purge simples"),
   nÃƒÂ£o por ordem alfabÃƒÂ©tica/arbitrÃƒÂ¡ria.
5. (peso 10%) NÃƒÂ£o reabre nenhuma decisÃƒÂ£o jÃƒÂ¡ `APPROVED` (D-066/D-067 duraÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o de 1800s, D-121/D-124
   mecanismo do orquestrador W3-07) Ã¢â‚¬â€ estende, nÃƒÂ£o substitui.
6. (peso 10%) Escopo de implementaÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o desta sessÃƒÂ£o ÃƒÂ© decidido explicitamente (design-only vs.
   tratÃƒÂ¡vel no mesmo dia) com critÃƒÂ©rio objetivo, nÃƒÂ£o "parece grande" sem justificativa.
```

## Proposta

### 1. Mecanismo de quarentena (cross-cutting)

**Novo estado `HELD_FOR_RECOVERY`, inserido entre `DELETING` e `QUIESCING`** no lifecycle de
qualquer entidade que declare "dado persistido + exclusÃƒÂ£o irreversÃƒÂ­vel" Ã¢â‚¬â€ hoje sÃƒÂ³
`TenantLifecycleRecord`/W3-07 tem esse lifecycle real; o desenho ÃƒÂ© feito para ser o padrÃƒÂ£o que a
futura feature de arquivos (e qualquer feature seguinte da mesma classe) reusa sem reinventar.

- **TransiÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o**: `ACTIVE Ã¢â€ â€™ HELD_FOR_RECOVERY` (era `ACTIVE Ã¢â€ â€™ DELETING` direto). `DELETING` passa a
  significar exclusividade "o operador confirmou, quarentena venceu, purga fÃƒÂ­sica em curso" Ã¢â‚¬â€ nÃƒÂ£o
  mais o primeiro passo.
- **DuraÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o**: **30 dias** Ã¢â‚¬â€ dentro da faixa convergente da pesquisa (20-90d), no ponto mÃƒÂ©dio,
  consistente com o prÃƒÂ³prio `privacy-lgpd.md` Ã‚Â§4 jÃƒÂ¡ usar "30 dias" como prazo padrÃƒÂ£o default em 2
  das 9 classes (`ACCOUNT_ACTIVE`, `CORE_USER_DATA`) Ã¢â‚¬â€ reuso de um nÃƒÂºmero que jÃƒÂ¡ ÃƒÂ© norma interna do
  projeto, nÃƒÂ£o um valor novo importado sem conexÃƒÂ£o com o resto do sistema.
- **AÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o real de cancelamento**: **existe** Ã¢â‚¬â€ novo `CancelOrganizationClosureService`/aÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o
  `organization:cancel-close` (mesmo tier `OWNER_ROLES` de `organization:close`), transiÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o
  `HELD_FOR_RECOVERY Ã¢â€ â€™ ACTIVE` (ÃƒÂºnica exceÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o ao "ACTIVE nunca ÃƒÂ© reentrada" documentado em
  `tenant-lifecycle-record.ts` Ã¢â‚¬â€ o comentÃƒÂ¡rio original previu certo o desenho prÃƒÂ©-quarentena, esta
  rodada o substitui deliberadamente, nÃƒÂ£o o contradiz por descuido). Sem essa aÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o real, a
  "quarentena" seria sÃƒÂ³ um atraso, nÃƒÂ£o uma janela de recuperaÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o de fato Ã¢â‚¬â€ o prÃƒÂ³prio motivo que
  levou Marcelo a levantar o achado.
- **ImplementaÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o AWS**: reusa o mesmo Step Functions jÃƒÂ¡ existente (`tenant-purge.asl.json`) Ã¢â‚¬â€ o
  `Wait(1800s)` atual desloca de posiÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o (passa a rodar DEPOIS de `HELD_FOR_RECOVERYÃ¢â€ â€™DELETING`,
  mantendo seu propÃƒÂ³sito operacional intacto) e um novo `Wait` de 30 dias (`Timestamp`/`Seconds:
  2592000` Ã¢â‚¬â€ Step Functions suporta waits de atÃƒÂ© 1 ano) entra antes. Nenhum novo serviÃƒÂ§o AWS.
- **GeneralizaÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o**: o par de estados (`HELD_FOR_RECOVERY` + duraÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o configurÃƒÂ¡vel + aÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o de cancel)
  ÃƒÂ© documentado como o padrÃƒÂ£o a seguir por qualquer lifecycle futuro de exclusÃƒÂ£o irreversÃƒÂ­vel Ã¢â‚¬â€ nÃƒÂ£o
  uma classe TypeScript compartilhada prematura (a feature de arquivos nem existe ainda para saber
  a forma real do reuso de cÃƒÂ³digo; documentar o PADRÃƒÆ’O agora, herdar a implementaÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o depois, ÃƒÂ©
  proporcional ao que se sabe hoje Ã¢â‚¬â€ mesmo raciocÃƒÂ­nio jÃƒÂ¡ usado em B2B-3 para adiar
  `ownerCount`).

### 2. PriorizaÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o das 7 classes LGPD sem purga fÃƒÂ­sica real

Ordem de prioridade (maior exposiÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o/sensibilidade primeiro), critÃƒÂ©rio: dado pessoal
identificÃƒÂ¡vel diretamente exposto a um titular vs. dado agregado/tÃƒÂ©cnico:

1. **`CORE_USER_DATA`** (itens/polÃƒÂ­ticas/ocorrÃƒÂªncias dentro de tenant `ACTIVE`) Ã¢â‚¬â€ maior volume de
   dado pessoal do produto, sem purga por idade hoje fora do fechamento de tenant.
2. **`DELIVERY_RECORD`** (intents/attempts, 180 dias) Ã¢â‚¬â€ prazo jÃƒÂ¡ definido e vencendo continuamente
   desde o M10 (import) e M7 (extraÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o) irem a produÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o; sem worker, o gap cresce todo dia.
3. **`SECURITY_AUDIT`** (AuditEvent/logs, 365 dias) Ã¢â‚¬â€ maior volume acumulado ao longo do tempo
   (evento por mutaÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o sensÃƒÂ­vel), mas exposiÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o pessoal menor que 1/2 (metadado de auditoria, nÃƒÂ£o
   o prÃƒÂ³prio dado de negÃƒÂ³cio).
4. **`LEGAL_EVIDENCE`** Ã¢â‚¬â€ sensibilidade alta, mas propositalmente a mais lenta de implementar
   (exige aprovaÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o jurÃƒÂ­dica/KMS independente/Object Lock por design jÃƒÂ¡ `APPROVED`) Ã¢â‚¬â€ nÃƒÂ£o ÃƒÂ©
   proporcional adiantar a implementaÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o tÃƒÂ©cnica antes da trava jurÃƒÂ­dica estar clara.
5. **`QUOTA_TELEMETRY`** Ã¢â‚¬â€ dado identificÃƒÂ¡vel mas de baixa sensibilidade (mÃƒÂ©trica de uso).
6. **`ACCOUNT_ACTIVE`** (fora do caso de fechamento de tenant) Ã¢â‚¬â€ na prÃƒÂ¡tica, "encerramento + 30
   dias" para `Membership`/`Invitation`/`Channel` individuais dentro de um tenant que segue ativo
   (ex. um convite expirado, um membro removido) ÃƒÂ© o gap real remanescente aqui, menor volume que
   os 5 acima.
7. **`TRANSIENT`** Ã¢â‚¬â€ a classe jÃƒÂ¡ tem um exemplo fÃƒÂ­sico real (`InvitationTokenPointer`); os
   remanescentes (`WebhookInbox`, `UploadSlot`) sÃƒÂ£o o menor risco (dado de trabalho de curtÃƒÂ­ssima
   vida, 7 dias/24h) Ã¢â‚¬â€ ÃƒÂºltima prioridade porque a exposiÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o residual ÃƒÂ© a mais baixa da lista.

### Escopo explÃƒÂ­cito de implementaÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o desta rodada

**Mecanismo de quarentena**: candidato a implementaÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o direta na mesma sessÃƒÂ£o se o design for
`APPROVED` Ã¢â‚¬â€ critÃƒÂ©rio objetivo: reusa a mÃƒÂ¡quina de estados/Lambdas jÃƒÂ¡ existentes do W3-07
(D-124), adiciona 1 estado + 1 `Wait` + 1 service novo (mesmo padrÃƒÂ£o de `CloseOrganizationService`)
+ 1 rota HTTP + 1 aÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o RBAC. Escopo comparÃƒÂ¡vel a D-125 (reatribuiÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o), que foi implementado no
mesmo dia do design.

**7 workers de purga LGPD por classe**: **fora de escopo de implementaÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o desta sessÃƒÂ£o** Ã¢â‚¬â€
7 workers reais (scan/scheduler/lifecycle por classe) ÃƒÂ© ordem de grandeza maior que qualquer wave
anterior implementada em um ÃƒÂºnico dia (compare com W3-06/`DocumentPurgeWorker`, que sozinho foi
uma wave dedicada). Esta rodada entrega design + priorizaÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o; implementaÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o fica para sessÃƒÂµes
futuras, uma classe por vez, na ordem acima Ã¢â‚¬â€ mesmo padrÃƒÂ£o usado para D-121Ã¢â€ â€™D-124 vs. deixar as 7
classes como trabalho futuro nomeado.

/**
 * CloseOrganizationService Ã¢â‚¬â€ W3-07 purge orchestrator (D-124, implementing D-121's approved
 * design, Rodada 2 Fix 1 with Rodada 3 Fix 8's corrected step ordering).
 *
 * The single real trigger surface for tenant deletion: moves the tenant's `TenantLifecycleRecord`
 * `ACTIVE -> DELETING` and launches the tenant-purge Step Functions execution that drives every
 * remaining transition. Before this service existed, both `transitionTenantLifecycle` and
 * `purgeTenant()` were real, working, and completely unreachable Ã¢â‚¬â€ nothing in production ever
 * called either.
 *
 * Step ordering is load-bearing and was corrected during the design protocol (Rodada 3 Fix 8): the
 * terminal-state check runs BEFORE the unconditional `StartExecution`, not after. Rodada 2's
 * original wording implied any post-`ACTIVE` status should trigger a fresh launch, which would
 * have re-launched executions for VERIFIED/DELETED/BLOCKED/HELD tenants Ã¢â‚¬â€ states where a purge
 * execution is either finished or deliberately parked awaiting an operator.
 *
 * `StartExecution` IS called unconditionally for the genuinely in-flight states
 * (`DELETING`/`QUIESCING`/`PURGING`), including when this call performed no write at all. That is
 * the established `start-extraction-run.ts` idiom, not an oversight: gating the launch on "did I
 * just write the record" orphans the tenant forever whenever the DynamoDB transition commits but
 * the subsequent `StartExecution` fails transiently Ã¢â‚¬â€ the record would sit in DELETING with no
 * execution behind it, and every retry would skip the launch because the write already happened.
 * Step Functions' own name-based uniqueness (`name: tenantId`) is what makes calling it every
 * time safe.
 */
import { authorize } from "../../identity/domain/authorization.js";
import type { RequestContext } from "../../identity/domain/request-context.js";
import { OrganizationClosureUnavailableError, NotFoundError } from "../../../shared/errors/app-error.js";
import { SystemMutationConflictError, transitionTenantLifecycle, type SystemMutationStore } from "../../../shared/tenant-lifecycle/system-mutation.js";
import type { TenantLifecycleRecord, TenantLifecycleStatus } from "../../../shared/tenant-lifecycle/tenant-lifecycle-record.js";
import type { TenantPurgeExecutionStarter } from "../../../shared/tenant-lifecycle/tenant-purge-execution-starter.js";

/** Same narrow read contract the orchestrator's transition handler uses Ã¢â‚¬â€ `ConsistentRead: true`
 * is mandatory, since this read produces the `expectedVersion` the OCC-fenced transition below is
 * conditioned on. */
export interface TenantLifecycleReader {
  read(tenantId: string): Promise<TenantLifecycleRecord | undefined>;
}

/** States where a purge is either already finished or parked awaiting a human Ã¢â‚¬â€ closing is
 * refused outright rather than re-launched. Named "unavailable", not "terminal": an operator can
 * still remediate BLOCKED/HELD (`tenant-lifecycle-record.ts`), so these are not all true terminal
 * states. */
const CLOSURE_UNAVAILABLE_STATUSES: ReadonlySet<TenantLifecycleStatus> = new Set(["VERIFIED", "DELETED", "BLOCKED", "HELD"]);

/** The genuinely in-flight states Ã¢â‚¬â€ a close() call landing here performs no write and only
 * repeats the idempotent launch. */
const IN_FLIGHT_STATUSES: ReadonlySet<TenantLifecycleStatus> = new Set(["DELETING", "QUIESCING", "PURGING"]);

export interface CloseOrganizationResult {
  tenantId: string;
  status: TenantLifecycleStatus;
  /** False when the record was already DELETING+ and this call only repeated the launch. */
  transitioned: boolean;
}

export class CloseOrganizationService {
  constructor(
    private readonly store: SystemMutationStore,
    private readonly reader: TenantLifecycleReader,
    private readonly executions: TenantPurgeExecutionStarter,
    private readonly tableName: string,
    private readonly now: () => string = () => new Date().toISOString(),
  ) {}

  async close(ctx: RequestContext): Promise<CloseOrganizationResult> {
    const tenantId = ctx.tenant.tenantId;
    authorize({ context: ctx, action: "organization:close", resource: { tenantId } });

    const record = await this.reader.read(tenantId);
    if (!record) {
      throw new NotFoundError("Organization lifecycle record not found.", { organizationId: tenantId });
    }
    if (CLOSURE_UNAVAILABLE_STATUSES.has(record.status)) {
      throw new OrganizationClosureUnavailableError(undefined, { organizationId: tenantId, status: record.status });
    }

    let transitioned = false;
    if (record.status === "ACTIVE") {
      try {
        await transitionTenantLifecycle({
          store: this.store,
          tableName: this.tableName,
          tenantId,
          from: "ACTIVE",
          to: "DELETING",
          expectedVersion: record.version,
          now: this.now,
        });
        transitioned = true;
      } catch (err) {
        if (!(err instanceof SystemMutationConflictError)) throw err;
        // A concurrent close() (double-click, retried request) won the race. Re-read once: if the
        // record legitimately landed on an in-flight state, this call simply joins that closure
        // and proceeds to the shared idempotent launch below. Anything else Ã¢â‚¬â€ still ACTIVE, or
        // raced into a stuck state Ã¢â‚¬â€ is genuinely unexpected and is not papered over.
        const after = await this.reader.read(tenantId);
        if (!after || !IN_FLIGHT_STATUSES.has(after.status)) throw err;
      }
    } else if (!IN_FLIGHT_STATUSES.has(record.status)) {
      // Exhaustiveness guard: ACTIVE, the 4 unavailable states, and the 3 in-flight states cover
      // every TenantLifecycleStatus. Reaching here means a status was added without deciding what
      // closing should do with it Ã¢â‚¬â€ fail loudly rather than silently launching an execution.
      throw new OrganizationClosureUnavailableError(undefined, { organizationId: tenantId, status: record.status });
    }

    await this.executions.startExecution({ name: tenantId, input: { tenantId } });

    return { tenantId, status: transitioned ? "DELETING" : record.status, transitioned };
  }
}

/**
 * TenantLifecycleRecord Ã¢â‚¬â€ W3-07 tenant deletion fence (D-067, `docs/architecture/decisions-log.md`;
 * design approved via Claude<->Codex protocol, 9.2/9.1, `docs/architecture/reviews/
 * w3-07-tenant-fence-round3-active-only-design/claude-analysis-active-only-fence.md` Ã‚Â§G/Ã‚Â§H).
 *
 * A tombstone item that outlives the tenant's own data cascade Ã¢â‚¬â€ same pattern as
 * `IdentityMapping` (identity-mapping-repository.ts): it lives outside the normal purge
 * cascade permanently, because it is the one artifact that must survive to prove "this
 * tenant existed and was deleted" and to block first-login from silently reprovisioning a
 * deleted tenant (the root cause the design doc traces D-063's failure to).
 *
 * Lives under `src/shared/` (not `src/modules/identity/domain/`) deliberately: it is
 * consumed by `src/shared/tenant-lifecycle/tenant-business-mutation.ts`, a shared/
 * infrastructure-level fencing primitive every tenant-scoped business mutation across
 * modules will eventually route through Ã¢â‚¬â€ and `shared/**` must never import from
 * `modules/**` (`.dependency-cruiser.cjs`'s `shared-must-not-reach-modules` rule). Placing
 * the record's own domain type here, instead of in `identity/domain` with the lane
 * importing it back out, keeps the dependency direction modules -> shared intact.
 */
import type { EntityKey } from "../dynamodb/occ.js";

/**
 * Forward-only state machine (approved design Ã‚Â§G):
 *   ACTIVE -> DELETING -> QUIESCING -> PURGING -> VERIFIED -> DELETED
 * Any of DELETING/QUIESCING/PURGING/VERIFIED can additionally move to BLOCKED/HELD (stuck
 * state Ã¢â‚¬â€ legal hold or a purge error that keeps failing) and back to the SAME state once
 * remediated. ACTIVE is never re-entered from any other state (no "un-delete") and DELETED
 * is a true terminal Ã¢â‚¬â€ nothing transitions out of it (the sweeper described in the design
 * doc repairs residue in place, it never re-opens the lifecycle record's status).
 */
export type TenantLifecycleStatus =
  | "ACTIVE"
  | "DELETING"
  | "QUIESCING"
  | "PURGING"
  | "VERIFIED"
  | "DELETED"
  | "BLOCKED"
  | "HELD";

export interface TenantLifecycleRecord extends EntityKey {
  SK: "LIFECYCLE";
  entityType: "TenantLifecycleRecord";
  tenantId: string;
  status: TenantLifecycleStatus;
  /** Set when entering BLOCKED/HELD and cleared on remediation back to the prior state Ã¢â‚¬â€ a
   * short machine-readable reason for the stuck state (e.g. "PURGE_S3_ERRORS",
   * "LEGAL_HOLD"), never free text containing tenant PII. */
  blockedReason?: string;
  /** The state BLOCKED/HELD was entered from, so remediation knows where to resume Ã¢â‚¬â€
   * required whenever status is BLOCKED/HELD, absent otherwise. */
  blockedFrom?: TenantLifecycleStatus;
  createdAt: string;
  updatedAt: string;
  version: number;
}

export function tenantLifecycleKey(tenantId: string): EntityKey {
  return { PK: `TENANT#${tenantId}#LIFECYCLE`, SK: "LIFECYCLE" };
}

/**
 * Statuses under which a `TenantBusinessMutation` may be admitted Ã¢â‚¬â€ deliberately just
 * `ACTIVE` (approved design Ã‚Â§H invariant 1: "Nenhuma mutaÃƒÂ§ÃƒÂ£o de negÃƒÂ³cio tenant-scoped
 * commita em DynamoDB sem ConditionCheck de TenantLifecycleRecord.status = ACTIVE").
 */
export const TENANT_ACTIVE_STATUS: TenantLifecycleStatus = "ACTIVE";

/** Forward-only transition graph. A state maps to the set of states it may legally move to
 * next; BLOCKED/HELD's own allowed targets depend on `blockedFrom` (checked separately by
 * `canResumeFromBlocked`, since the graph itself can't express "return to wherever you came
 * from" as a fixed edge list). */
const FORWARD_TRANSITIONS: Record<TenantLifecycleStatus, ReadonlySet<TenantLifecycleStatus>> = {
  ACTIVE: new Set(["DELETING"]),
  DELETING: new Set(["QUIESCING", "BLOCKED", "HELD"]),
  QUIESCING: new Set(["PURGING", "BLOCKED", "HELD"]),
  PURGING: new Set(["VERIFIED", "BLOCKED", "HELD"]),
  VERIFIED: new Set(["DELETED", "BLOCKED", "HELD"]),
  DELETED: new Set([]),
  BLOCKED: new Set([]), // only canResumeFromBlocked() below, not a fixed forward edge
  HELD: new Set([]),
};

/**
 * True if `from -> to` is a legal forward transition, OR a remediation resume out of
 * BLOCKED/HELD back to the exact state it was blocked from. Never true for anything that
 * would re-enter ACTIVE or leave DELETED Ã¢â‚¬â€ those two guarantees are the entire point of
 * this fence (approved design Ã‚Â§H invariant 4, Ã‚Â§Q "no ACTIVE reversion, no DELETED
 * resurrection").
 */
export function canTransition(from: TenantLifecycleStatus, to: TenantLifecycleStatus, blockedFrom?: TenantLifecycleStatus): boolean {
  if (to === "ACTIVE") return false; // never re-enter ACTIVE from anywhere
  if (from === "DELETED") return false; // DELETED is terminal, nothing leaves it
  if ((from === "BLOCKED" || from === "HELD") && blockedFrom) {
    return to === blockedFrom;
  }
  return FORWARD_TRANSITIONS[from]?.has(to) ?? false;
}

export class InvalidTenantLifecycleTransitionError extends Error {
  constructor(
    public readonly from: TenantLifecycleStatus,
    public readonly to: TenantLifecycleStatus,
  ) {
    super(`Invalid TenantLifecycleRecord transition: ${from} -> ${to}`);
    this.name = "InvalidTenantLifecycleTransitionError";
  }
}

/** Throws InvalidTenantLifecycleTransitionError instead of returning false Ã¢â‚¬â€ for callers
 * (e.g. the future lifecycle-transition worker) that want a fail-fast assertion rather than
 * a boolean they must remember to check. */
export function assertValidTransition(from: TenantLifecycleStatus, to: TenantLifecycleStatus, blockedFrom?: TenantLifecycleStatus): void {
  if (!canTransition(from, to, blockedFrom)) {
    throw new InvalidTenantLifecycleTransitionError(from, to);
  }
}

43:  acessado 2026-08-31): usuÃ¡rio deletado Ã© restaurÃ¡vel por administrador por **20 dias**; dados de
44:  Drive/Gmail especificamente por atÃ© 25 dias. Janela mais curta que GitHub/AWS, mesma famÃ­lia de
45:  padrÃ£o (aÃ§Ã£o administrativa explÃ­cita de restauraÃ§Ã£o dentro de um prazo fixo, depois disso

48:  2026-08-31): arquivos sem compartilhamento tÃªm grace period de **30 dias** antes de apagados

50:
51:ConvergÃªncia real: 3 de 4 fontes (GitHub, AWS, e o precedente-irmÃ£o Slack-arquivo) usam algo entre
52:30-90 dias; Google Workspace Ã© o outlier mais curto (20-25 dias), mas ainda na mesma ordem de

55:para o problema operacional distinto de drenar requests em voo).

78:## Proposta

80:### 1. Mecanismo de quarentena (cross-cutting)

1:/**
2: * CloseOrganizationService â€” W3-07 purge orchestrator (D-124, implementing D-121's approved
3: * design, Rodada 2 Fix 1 with Rodada 3 Fix 8's corrected step ordering).
4: *
5: * The single real trigger surface for tenant deletion: moves the tenant's `TenantLifecycleRecord`
6: * `ACTIVE -> DELETING` and launches the tenant-purge Step Functions execution that drives every
7: * remaining transition. Before this service existed, both `transitionTenantLifecycle` and
8: * `purgeTenant()` were real, working, and completely unreachable â€” nothing in production ever
9: * called either.
10: *
11: * Step ordering is load-bearing and was corrected during the design protocol (Rodada 3 Fix 8): the
12: * terminal-state check runs BEFORE the unconditional `StartExecution`, not after. Rodada 2's
13: * original wording implied any post-`ACTIVE` status should trigger a fresh launch, which would
14: * have re-launched executions for VERIFIED/DELETED/BLOCKED/HELD tenants â€” states where a purge
15: * execution is either finished or deliberately parked awaiting an operator.
16: *
17: * `StartExecution` IS called unconditionally for the genuinely in-flight states
18: * (`DELETING`/`QUIESCING`/`PURGING`), including when this call performed no write at all. That is
19: * the established `start-extraction-run.ts` idiom, not an oversight: gating the launch on "did I
20: * just write the record" orphans the tenant forever whenever the DynamoDB transition commits but
21: * the subsequent `StartExecution` fails transiently â€” the record would sit in DELETING with no
22: * execution behind it, and every retry would skip the launch because the write already happened.
23: * Step Functions' own name-based uniqueness (`name: tenantId`) is what makes calling it every
24: * time safe.
25: */
26:import { authorize } from "../../identity/domain/authorization.js";
27:import type { RequestContext } from "../../identity/domain/request-context.js";
28:import { OrganizationClosureUnavailableError, NotFoundError } from "../../../shared/errors/app-error.js";
29:import { SystemMutationConflictError, transitionTenantLifecycle, type SystemMutationStore } from "../../../shared/tenant-lifecycle/system-mutation.js";
30:import type { TenantLifecycleRecord, TenantLifecycleStatus } from "../../../shared/tenant-lifecycle/tenant-lifecycle-record.js";
31:import type { TenantPurgeExecutionStarter } from "../../../shared/tenant-lifecycle/tenant-purge-execution-starter.js";
32:
33:/** Same narrow read contract the orchestrator's transition handler uses â€” `ConsistentRead: true`
34: * is mandatory, since this read produces the `expectedVersion` the OCC-fenced transition below is
35: * conditioned on. */
36:export interface TenantLifecycleReader {
37:  read(tenantId: string): Promise<TenantLifecycleRecord | undefined>;
38:}
39:
40:/** States where a purge is either already finished or parked awaiting a human â€” closing is
41: * refused outright rather than re-launched. Named "unavailable", not "terminal": an operator can
42: * still remediate BLOCKED/HELD (`tenant-lifecycle-record.ts`), so these are not all true terminal
43: * states. */
44:const CLOSURE_UNAVAILABLE_STATUSES: ReadonlySet<TenantLifecycleStatus> = new Set(["VERIFIED", "DELETED", "BLOCKED", "HELD"]);
45:
46:/** The genuinely in-flight states â€” a close() call landing here performs no write and only
47: * repeats the idempotent launch. */
48:const IN_FLIGHT_STATUSES: ReadonlySet<TenantLifecycleStatus> = new Set(["DELETING", "QUIESCING", "PURGING"]);
49:
50:export interface CloseOrganizationResult {
51:  tenantId: string;
52:  status: TenantLifecycleStatus;
53:  /** False when the record was already DELETING+ and this call only repeated the launch. */
54:  transitioned: boolean;
55:}
56:
57:export class CloseOrganizationService {
58:  constructor(
59:    private readonly store: SystemMutationStore,
60:    private readonly reader: TenantLifecycleReader,
61:    private readonly executions: TenantPurgeExecutionStarter,
62:    private readonly tableName: string,
63:    private readonly now: () => string = () => new Date().toISOString(),
64:  ) {}
65:
66:  async close(ctx: RequestContext): Promise<CloseOrganizationResult> {
67:    const tenantId = ctx.tenant.tenantId;
68:    authorize({ context: ctx, action: "organization:close", resource: { tenantId } });
69:
70:    const record = await this.reader.read(tenantId);
71:    if (!record) {
72:      throw new NotFoundError("Organization lifecycle record not found.", { organizationId: tenantId });
73:    }
74:    if (CLOSURE_UNAVAILABLE_STATUSES.has(record.status)) {
75:      throw new OrganizationClosureUnavailableError(undefined, { organizationId: tenantId, status: record.status });
76:    }
77:
78:    let transitioned = false;
79:    if (record.status === "ACTIVE") {
80:      try {
81:        await transitionTenantLifecycle({
82:          store: this.store,
83:          tableName: this.tableName,
84:          tenantId,
85:          from: "ACTIVE",
86:          to: "DELETING",
87:          expectedVersion: record.version,
88:          now: this.now,
89:        });
90:        transitioned = true;
91:      } catch (err) {
92:        if (!(err instanceof SystemMutationConflictError)) throw err;
93:        // A concurrent close() (double-click, retried request) won the race. Re-read once: if the
94:        // record legitimately landed on an in-flight state, this call simply joins that closure
95:        // and proceeds to the shared idempotent launch below. Anything else â€” still ACTIVE, or
96:        // raced into a stuck state â€” is genuinely unexpected and is not papered over.
97:        const after = await this.reader.read(tenantId);
98:        if (!after || !IN_FLIGHT_STATUSES.has(after.status)) throw err;
99:      }
100:    } else if (!IN_FLIGHT_STATUSES.has(record.status)) {
101:      // Exhaustiveness guard: ACTIVE, the 4 unavailable states, and the 3 in-flight states cover
102:      // every TenantLifecycleStatus. Reaching here means a status was added without deciding what
103:      // closing should do with it â€” fail loudly rather than silently launching an execution.
104:      throw new OrganizationClosureUnavailableError(undefined, { organizationId: tenantId, status: record.status });
105:    }
106:
107:    await this.executions.startExecution({ name: tenantId, input: { tenantId } });
108:
109:    return { tenantId, status: transitioned ? "DELETING" : record.status, transitioned };
110:  }
111:}
1:/**
2: * TenantLifecycleRecord â€” W3-07 tenant deletion fence (D-067, `docs/architecture/decisions-log.md`;
3: * design approved via Claude<->Codex protocol, 9.2/9.1, `docs/architecture/reviews/
4: * w3-07-tenant-fence-round3-active-only-design/claude-analysis-active-only-fence.md` Â§G/Â§H).
5: *
6: * A tombstone item that outlives the tenant's own data cascade â€” same pattern as
7: * `IdentityMapping` (identity-mapping-repository.ts): it lives outside the normal purge
8: * cascade permanently, because it is the one artifact that must survive to prove "this
9: * tenant existed and was deleted" and to block first-login from silently reprovisioning a
10: * deleted tenant (the root cause the design doc traces D-063's failure to).
11: *
12: * Lives under `src/shared/` (not `src/modules/identity/domain/`) deliberately: it is
13: * consumed by `src/shared/tenant-lifecycle/tenant-business-mutation.ts`, a shared/
14: * infrastructure-level fencing primitive every tenant-scoped business mutation across
15: * modules will eventually route through â€” and `shared/**` must never import from
16: * `modules/**` (`.dependency-cruiser.cjs`'s `shared-must-not-reach-modules` rule). Placing
17: * the record's own domain type here, instead of in `identity/domain` with the lane
18: * importing it back out, keeps the dependency direction modules -> shared intact.
19: */
20:import type { EntityKey } from "../dynamodb/occ.js";
21:
22:/**
23: * Forward-only state machine (approved design Â§G):
24: *   ACTIVE -> DELETING -> QUIESCING -> PURGING -> VERIFIED -> DELETED
25: * Any of DELETING/QUIESCING/PURGING/VERIFIED can additionally move to BLOCKED/HELD (stuck
26: * state â€” legal hold or a purge error that keeps failing) and back to the SAME state once
27: * remediated. ACTIVE is never re-entered from any other state (no "un-delete") and DELETED
28: * is a true terminal â€” nothing transitions out of it (the sweeper described in the design
29: * doc repairs residue in place, it never re-opens the lifecycle record's status).
30: */
31:export type TenantLifecycleStatus =
32:  | "ACTIVE"
33:  | "DELETING"
34:  | "QUIESCING"
35:  | "PURGING"
36:  | "VERIFIED"
37:  | "DELETED"
38:  | "BLOCKED"
39:  | "HELD";
40:
41:export interface TenantLifecycleRecord extends EntityKey {
42:  SK: "LIFECYCLE";
43:  entityType: "TenantLifecycleRecord";
44:  tenantId: string;
45:  status: TenantLifecycleStatus;
46:  /** Set when entering BLOCKED/HELD and cleared on remediation back to the prior state â€” a
47:   * short machine-readable reason for the stuck state (e.g. "PURGE_S3_ERRORS",
48:   * "LEGAL_HOLD"), never free text containing tenant PII. */
49:  blockedReason?: string;
50:  /** The state BLOCKED/HELD was entered from, so remediation knows where to resume â€”
51:   * required whenever status is BLOCKED/HELD, absent otherwise. */
52:  blockedFrom?: TenantLifecycleStatus;
53:  createdAt: string;
54:  updatedAt: string;
55:  version: number;
56:}
57:
58:export function tenantLifecycleKey(tenantId: string): EntityKey {
59:  return { PK: `TENANT#${tenantId}#LIFECYCLE`, SK: "LIFECYCLE" };
60:}
61:
62:/**
63: * Statuses under which a `TenantBusinessMutation` may be admitted â€” deliberately just
64: * `ACTIVE` (approved design Â§H invariant 1: "Nenhuma mutaÃ§Ã£o de negÃ³cio tenant-scoped
65: * commita em DynamoDB sem ConditionCheck de TenantLifecycleRecord.status = ACTIVE").
66: */
67:export const TENANT_ACTIVE_STATUS: TenantLifecycleStatus = "ACTIVE";
68:
69:/** Forward-only transition graph. A state maps to the set of states it may legally move to
70: * next; BLOCKED/HELD's own allowed targets depend on `blockedFrom` (checked separately by
71: * `canResumeFromBlocked`, since the graph itself can't express "return to wherever you came
72: * from" as a fixed edge list). */
73:const FORWARD_TRANSITIONS: Record<TenantLifecycleStatus, ReadonlySet<TenantLifecycleStatus>> = {
74:  ACTIVE: new Set(["DELETING"]),
75:  DELETING: new Set(["QUIESCING", "BLOCKED", "HELD"]),
76:  QUIESCING: new Set(["PURGING", "BLOCKED", "HELD"]),
77:  PURGING: new Set(["VERIFIED", "BLOCKED", "HELD"]),
78:  VERIFIED: new Set(["DELETED", "BLOCKED", "HELD"]),
79:  DELETED: new Set([]),
80:  BLOCKED: new Set([]), // only canResumeFromBlocked() below, not a fixed forward edge
81:  HELD: new Set([]),
82:};
83:
84:/**
85: * True if `from -> to` is a legal forward transition, OR a remediation resume out of
86: * BLOCKED/HELD back to the exact state it was blocked from. Never true for anything that
87: * would re-enter ACTIVE or leave DELETED â€” those two guarantees are the entire point of
88: * this fence (approved design Â§H invariant 4, Â§Q "no ACTIVE reversion, no DELETED
89: * resurrection").
90: */
91:export function canTransition(from: TenantLifecycleStatus, to: TenantLifecycleStatus, blockedFrom?: TenantLifecycleStatus): boolean {
92:  if (to === "ACTIVE") return false; // never re-enter ACTIVE from anywhere
93:  if (from === "DELETED") return false; // DELETED is terminal, nothing leaves it
94:  if ((from === "BLOCKED" || from === "HELD") && blockedFrom) {
95:    return to === blockedFrom;
96:  }
97:  return FORWARD_TRANSITIONS[from]?.has(to) ?? false;
98:}
99:
100:export class InvalidTenantLifecycleTransitionError extends Error {
101:  constructor(
102:    public readonly from: TenantLifecycleStatus,
103:    public readonly to: TenantLifecycleStatus,
104:  ) {
105:    super(`Invalid TenantLifecycleRecord transition: ${from} -> ${to}`);
106:    this.name = "InvalidTenantLifecycleTransitionError";
107:  }
108:}
109:
110:/** Throws InvalidTenantLifecycleTransitionError instead of returning false â€” for callers
111: * (e.g. the future lifecycle-transition worker) that want a fail-fast assertion rather than
112: * a boolean they must remember to check. */
113:export function assertValidTransition(from: TenantLifecycleStatus, to: TenantLifecycleStatus, blockedFrom?: TenantLifecycleStatus): void {
114:  if (!canTransition(from, to, blockedFrom)) {
115:    throw new InvalidTenantLifecycleTransitionError(from, to);
116:  }
117:}

61:## `reviews/` â€” convenÃ§Ã£o de nomenclatura e classificaÃ§Ã£o
62:
63:EvidÃªncia de rodada, mesmo papel que `docs/architecture/history/` tem para decisÃµes de arquitetura: nunca normativo, sÃ³ prova de como uma nota/decisÃ£o foi alcanÃ§ada. TrÃªs geraÃ§Ãµes de conteÃºdo coexistem aqui, nesta ordem cronolÃ³gica:
64:
65:1. **Checkpoints da Engineering Maturity Review** (`checkpoint-01-rubric/`, `checkpoint-02-09-consolidated/`, `checkpoint-12-redteam/`, e os arquivos soltos `_codex-*-checkpoint1-*.txt` no nÃ­vel raiz de `reviews/`) â€” produziram a rubrica CONGELADA (`01-engineering-quality-criteria.md`) e o red team que motivou G8. 66:2. **ConvergÃªncia dos critÃ©rios por eixo** (`joint-review-criteria-round1-*`, `security-axis-criteria-round1-*`, `remaining-axes-round1-*`, `audit-areas-*`) â€” produziram `joint-review-criteria.md`.
67:3. **Full-audit dos 9 eixos formais** (`full-audit-round1-<eixo>-*`) â€” a rodada em andamento nesta sessÃ£o. PadrÃ£o de nomenclatura por eixo (`<eixo>` âˆˆ `arquitetura`, `qualidade`, `contexto`, `seguranca`, ...):
68:   - `full-audit-round1-<eixo>-claude.md`, `-claude-round2.md`, `-claude-round3.md`, ... â€” nota cega do Claude, uma por rodada.
69:   - `full-audit-round1-<eixo>-codex-prompt.txt` / `-codex-prompt-round2.txt`, ... â€” prompt enviado ao Codex CLI, um por rodada.
70:   - `full-audit-round1-<eixo>-codex-output-round1.txt`, `-round2.txt`, ... â€” saÃ­da bruta do Codex CLI. 71:   - `full-audit-round1-<eixo>-summary.md` â€” **o Ãºnico arquivo desta trinca que deveria ser lido diretamente.** Nota final por critÃ©rio (ambos os lados), achados corrigidos com commit real, achados restantes classificados em "impedimento externo real" vs. "escopo maior que correÃ§Ã£o pontual".
72:
73:Arquivos de transcript bruto (`.txt`, prefixo `_`, ou os `full-audit-*-codex-output-*.txt`) nunca sÃ£o fonte de verdade por si sÃ³ â€” sempre prefira o `.md` de resumo/nota que os acompanha. **PolÃ­tica de proveniÃªncia/metadata**: os `.md` de nota/resumo carregam frontmatter (`status`/`owner`/`authority`, mais `Last verified` nos routers); os `.txt` brutos nÃ£o â€” exigir frontmatter em cada transcript violaria proporcionalidade (`principles.md` #1) sem ganho real, jÃ¡ que sua proveniÃªncia Ã© herdada do `.md` que os acompanha (mesmo prefixo de nome) e da posiÃ§Ã£o cronolÃ³gica descrita nesta seÃ§Ã£o. 108-estado terminal `DELETED`) jÃ¡ Ã© tratada por `resolveWorkingOrganization()` como indisponÃ­vel,
109-disparando a mesma auto-cura de sessÃ£o (limpa `activeOrganizationId` stale, recalcula
110-`organizationSelectionRequired`/auto-seleciona a Ãºnica organizaÃ§Ã£o usÃ¡vel restante) â€” provado por
111-teste nomeando explicitamente o estado terminal `DELETED`, nÃ£o sÃ³ `DELETING`.
112-
113-## 5. Subprocessadores e transferÃªncia internacional (PRIV-005/007)

109:/**
110: * Role model closed by Wave B2B-7 (RBAC, D-097/D-098) â€” `Membership.role` (D-090) has 4 real
111: * values; this matrix now recognizes all of them. Research protocol E-014 applied for the
112: * first time (docs/engineering/research-protocol.md): GitHub/Linear/Slack/Notion + NIST/ANSI
113: * INCITS 359 (Hierarchical RBAC) don't converge on a single "what does Admin get beyond
114: * Member" answer, so each action formerly gated to ADMIN_ROLES got a named, individual
115: * decision instead of a blanket parity rule â€” see multi-user-b2b-wave-b2b7-scope.md.
116: */
117:export type Role = "OWNER" | "ADMIN" | "MEMBER" | "VIEWER";
118:
119:const READ_ONLY_ROLES: ReadonlySet<Role> = new Set(["OWNER", "ADMIN", "MEMBER", "VIEWER"]);
120:const WRITE_ROLES: ReadonlySet<Role> = new Set(["OWNER", "ADMIN", "MEMBER"]);
121:const ADMIN_ROLES: ReadonlySet<Role> = new Set(["OWNER", "ADMIN"]);
122:/** Owner-exclusive tier (B2B-7) â€” reserved for actions with tenant-wide external/reputational
123: * impact (research: the class GitHub/Linear/Slack/Notion keep apart from ordinary content
124: * admin). Members: `tenant:configure-document-request-delivery` (B2B-7),
125: * `organization:update-settings` (B2B-10), `organization:close` (W3-07/D-124). */
126:const OWNER_ROLES: ReadonlySet<Role> = new Set(["OWNER"]);
127:
128:const ACTION_ROLES: Record<Action, ReadonlySet<Role>> = {
129:  "item:create": WRITE_ROLES,
130:  "item:read": READ_ONLY_ROLES,
131:  "item:update": WRITE_ROLES,
132:  "item:delete": ADMIN_ROLES,
133:  "item:export": ADMIN_ROLES,
134:  "item:watch": WRITE_ROLES,
135:  "reminder:manage": WRITE_ROLES,
136:  "document:reserve-upload": WRITE_ROLES,
137:  "document:read": READ_ONLY_ROLES,
138:  "document:delete": ADMIN_ROLES,
139:  "extraction:confirm": WRITE_ROLES,
140:  // B2B-7 bug fix (not an ADMIN-vs-OWNER call): this action gates both read and update of a
141:  // per-user preference (ctx.principal.userId-keyed, notification-preferences-service.ts),
142:  // never tenant-wide config. Unlike profile:update (WRITE_ROLES, D-060 - tied to the ability
143:  // to ACT, since only a role that can create a RequirementAssignment needs a display name),
144:  // this is tied to the ability to RECEIVE a reminder - assigneeUserId is never role-checked,
145:  // so a VIEWER can legitimately be a notification recipient and must be able to configure it
146:  // for themself. Reuses READ_ONLY_ROLES (already "any real Membership") rather than adding a
147:  // 5th constant with the same 4 members.
148:  "notification:configure": READ_ONLY_ROLES,
149:  "audit:read": READ_ONLY_ROLES,
150:  "system:ping": READ_ONLY_ROLES,
151:  "subject:create": WRITE_ROLES,
152:  "subject:read": READ_ONLY_ROLES,
153:  "subject:update": WRITE_ROLES,
154:  "subject:delete": ADMIN_ROLES,
155:  "requirement:assign": WRITE_ROLES,
156:  "requirement:read": READ_ONLY_ROLES,
157:  "requirement:update": WRITE_ROLES,
158:  "requirement:delete": ADMIN_ROLES,
159:  "requirement:review": WRITE_ROLES,
160:  "requirement:request-document": WRITE_ROLES,
161:  "tenant:configure-document-request-delivery": OWNER_ROLES,
162:  "import:create": WRITE_ROLES,
163:  "import:read": READ_ONLY_ROLES,
164:  "import:commit": WRITE_ROLES,
165:  "profile:read": READ_ONLY_ROLES,
166:  "profile:update": WRITE_ROLES,
167:  "membership:invite": ADMIN_ROLES,
168:  "membership:revoke-invitation": ADMIN_ROLES,
169:  "membership:list-invitations": ADMIN_ROLES,
170:  "membership:list-members": READ_ONLY_ROLES,
171:  "membership:role-change": ADMIN_ROLES,
172:  "membership:remove": ADMIN_ROLES,
173:  "membership:leave": READ_ONLY_ROLES,
174:  "organization:update-settings": OWNER_ROLES,
175:  "organization:close": OWNER_ROLES,

178:export type AuthorizationDenialReason = "TENANT_MISMATCH" | "NO_MEMBERSHIP" | "INSUFFICIENT_ROLE" | "RESOURCE_OWNERSHIP_MISMATCH";
179:
180:export class AuthorizationDeniedError extends Error {
181:  constructor(
182:    readonly reason: AuthorizationDenialReason,
183:    readonly action: Action,
184:  ) {
185:    super(`Authorization denied for action "${action}": ${reason}`);
186:    this.name = "AuthorizationDeniedError";
187:  }
188:}
189:
190:/**
191: * authorize() â€” implementation-blueprint.md Â§4.3: "authorize() verifica primeiro
192: * igualdade de tenant, depois papel e vÃ­nculo por recurso." Throws
193: * AuthorizationDeniedError (never returns false) so callers cannot forget to check
194: * a boolean result â€” the http layer maps this to AuthorizationError (403).
195: *
196: * Order matters and is deliberately fail-closed at every step:
197: *  1. context must carry at least one role (no membership => no access at all).
198: *  2. if a resource is supplied, resource.tenantId MUST equal context.tenant.tenantId â€”
199: *     this is the check that makes cross-tenant ID substitution fail, independent of
200: *     role, and independent of what the caller *claims* the resource's tenant is (the
201: *     resource object here must always be populated from a DB read keyed by the
202: *     authenticated tenantId, never from client input â€” see repository contract).
203: *  3. role must be sufficient for the action.
204: *  4. if the resource declares an owner/assignee, membership role alone isn't enough
205: *     for actions that are resource-scoped by ownership in the future; MVP has no
206: *     per-item ownership finer than tenant, so this step is a no-op today but kept
207: *     explicit so a future item-level ACL doesn't require re-deriving this function.
208: */
209:export function authorize(input: AuthorizationInput): void {
210:  const { context, action, resource } = input;
211:  const roles = context.tenant.roles as Role[];
212:
213:  if (roles.length === 0) {
214:    throw new AuthorizationDeniedError("NO_MEMBERSHIP", action);
215:  }
216:
217:  if (resource && resource.tenantId !== context.tenant.tenantId) {
218:    throw new AuthorizationDeniedError("TENANT_MISMATCH", action);
219:  }
220:
221:  const allowedRoles = ACTION_ROLES[action];
222:  const hasRole = roles.some((role) => allowedRoles.has(role));
223:  if (!hasRole) {
224:    throw new AuthorizationDeniedError("INSUFFICIENT_ROLE", action);
225:  }
226:
227:  if (resource?.assigneeUserId && resource.ownerUserId) {
228:    const isOwnerOrAssignee =
229:      resource.ownerUserId === context.principal.userId ||
230:      resource.assigneeUserId === context.principal.userId;
231:    // OWNER and ADMIN bypass per-resource ownership (tenant-wide content admin, B2B-7 -
232:    // ADMIN has parity with OWNER over business resources, see authorization.ts role model
233:    // comment above), MEMBER/VIEWER scoped resources require ownership match once per-item
234:    // ACLs exist. This is enforced only when both fields ARE present (defensive for future
235:    // use), never invented from nothing.
236:    if (!roles.includes("OWNER") && !roles.includes("ADMIN") && !isOwnerOrAssignee) {
237:      throw new AuthorizationDeniedError("RESOURCE_OWNERSHIP_MISMATCH", action);
238:    }
239:  }
240:}
1:/**
2: * resolveRequestContext â€” Multi-User B2B, docs/architecture/multi-user-b2b-physical-model.md
3: * Â§11. Steps:
4: *  1. validation already performed by the API Gateway authorizer (JWT signature/exp) â€” this
5: *     function receives already-validated claims, it does not verify JWT signatures;
6: *  2. bootstrap the global identity (`IdentityBootstrapService`, 2-item atomic create) â€”
7: *     authentication no longer creates any tenant/Organization;
8: *  3. classify onboarding state (`OnboardingStateResolver`, Wave B2B-4/D-094) â€” only
9: *     `HAS_USABLE_MEMBERSHIP` proceeds to a working `RequestContext`, everything else throws
10: *     `OnboardingRequiredError` carrying the exact state;
11: *  4. resolve the working `Membership` â€” `organizationIdHint` (from `X-Organization-Id`,
12: *     Wave B2B-6/D-101) when present, else the (today) single `ACTIVE` Membership; both paths
13: *     revalidate via `resolveWorkingOrganization()` (Membership ACTIVE + the Organization's own
14: *     `TenantLifecycleRecord` ACTIVE â€” Â§11's chain ends "-> TenantLifecycleRecord ACTIVE ->
15: *     RequestContext", an ACTIVE Membership alone says nothing about the Organization's own
16: *     lifecycle);
17: *  5. reject tokens issued before globalLogoutAfter/deviceLogoutAfter (now user-global, Â§10);
18: *  6. build the immutable RequestContext.
19: */
20:import { AuthenticationError, InternalError, OnboardingRequiredError, OrganizationSelectionRequiredError, OrganizationUnavailableError, UnsupportedMembershipRoleError } from "../../../shared/errors/app-error.js";
21:import { UserRepository } from "../persistence/user-repository.js";
22:import { GlobalUserRepository } from "../persistence/global-user-repository.js";
23:import { IdentityBootstrapService } from "./bootstrap-identity.js";
24:import { OnboardingStateResolver } from "../../organization/application/onboarding-state.js";
25:import { resolveActiveMembership as resolveActiveMemberships } from "../../organization/application/resolve-active-membership.js";
26:import { resolveWorkingOrganization } from "../../organization/application/resolve-working-organization.js";
27:import type { Membership } from "../../organization/domain/membership.js";
28:import type { OrganizationStore } from "../../organization/ports/organization-store.js";
29:import type { IdentityStore } from "../ports/identity-store.js";
30:import type { RequestContext } from "../domain/request-context.js";

32:/** Claims already validated (signature + expiry) by the API Gateway JWT authorizer. */
33:export interface ValidatedClaims {
34:  sub: string;
35:  tokenId: string; // jti
36:  issuedAt: string; // iat, ISO-8601
37:  expiresAt: string; // exp, ISO-8601
38:  deviceId?: string;
39:}
40:
41:export interface ResolveRequestContextInput {
42:  claims: ValidatedClaims;
43:  requestId: string;
44:  correlationId: string;
45:  /** From `X-Organization-Id` (BFF-derived, Wave B2B-6/D-101) â€” REQUIRED (not `?:`), even
46:   * though `undefined` is a valid value, so the compiler blocks every real call site (56 across
47:   * 13 HTTP handler files, `grep -rl` verified) from silently forgetting to thread it through. */
48:  organizationIdHint: string | undefined;
49:}
50:
51:export interface IdGenerator {
52:  newUserId(): string;
53:  newSessionId(): string;
54:}
55:
56:export class RequestContextResolver {
57:  private readonly bootstrap: IdentityBootstrapService;
58:  private readonly onboarding: OnboardingStateResolver;
59:
60:  constructor(
61:    private readonly users: UserRepository,
62:    private readonly globalUsers: GlobalUserRepository,
63:    private readonly organizations: OrganizationStore,
64:    private readonly ids: IdGenerator,
65:    store: IdentityStore,
66:    tableName: string,
67:  ) {
68:    this.bootstrap = new IdentityBootstrapService(store, tableName);
69:    this.onboarding = new OnboardingStateResolver(organizations);
70:  }
71:
72:  async resolve(input: ResolveRequestContextInput): Promise<RequestContext> {
73:    const { claims } = input;
74:
75:    const newUserId = this.ids.newUserId();
76:    const { user } = await this.bootstrap.bootstrapUser(claims.sub, newUserId);
77:
78:    if (user.identityStatus !== "ACTIVE") {
79:      throw new AuthenticationError("User is not active.", { userId: user.userId });
80:    }
81:
82:    // Step 5 (user-global watermark, Â§10 â€” was UserProfile.globalLogoutAfter pre-cutover).
83:    if (user.globalLogoutAfter && claims.issuedAt < user.globalLogoutAfter) {
84:      throw new AuthenticationError("Session revoked (global logout).", { userId: user.userId });
85:    }
86:
87:    let deviceSession;
88:    if (claims.deviceId) {
89:      deviceSession = await this.globalUsers.getDeviceSession(user.userId, claims.deviceId);
90:      if (deviceSession) {
91:        if (deviceSession.status === "REVOKED") {
92:          throw new AuthenticationError("Session revoked (device logout).", { deviceId: claims.deviceId });
93:        }
94:        if (deviceSession.deviceLogoutAfter && claims.issuedAt < deviceSession.deviceLogoutAfter) {
95:          throw new AuthenticationError("Session revoked (device logout).", { deviceId: claims.deviceId });
96:        }
97:      }
98:    }
99:    const sessionId = deviceSession?.sessionId ?? this.ids.newSessionId();
100:
101:    const onboardingState = await this.onboarding.resolve(user.userId);
102:    if (onboardingState !== "HAS_USABLE_MEMBERSHIP") {
103:      throw new OnboardingRequiredError(onboardingState, { userId: user.userId });
104:    }
105:
106:    const membership = await this.resolveActiveMembership(user.userId, input.organizationIdHint);
107:
108:    const roles = this.resolveRoles(membership.role);
109:
110:    // ProfileService's invariant ("a UserProfile is guaranteed to already exist by the time any
111:    // RequestContext resolves") must keep holding post-cutover, now per-Organization instead of
112:    // per-legacy-tenant â€” provisioned lazily here, the first time this (organizationId, userId)
113:    // pair resolves, instead of at bootstrap time (no Organization is known yet at bootstrap).
114:    await this.users.createProfileIfAbsent({
115:      tenantId: membership.organizationId,
116:      userId: user.userId,
117:      identitySubject: claims.sub,
118:      emailNormalized: user.emailNormalized,
119:      roles,
120:      status: "ACTIVE",
121:    });
122:
123:    return {
124:      requestId: input.requestId,
125:      correlationId: input.correlationId,
126:      principal: {
127:        userId: user.userId,
128:        cognitoSubject: claims.sub,
129:        sessionId,
130:        deviceId: claims.deviceId,
131:      },
132:      tenant: {
133:        tenantId: membership.organizationId,
134:        membershipId: membership.membershipId,
135:        roles,
136:      },
137:      auth: {
138:        issuedAt: claims.issuedAt,
139:        expiresAt: claims.expiresAt,
140:        tokenId: claims.tokenId,
141:      },
142:    };
143:  }
144:
145:  /**
146:   * Physical model Â§11's resolution chain: `... -> BFF session.activeOrganizationId (seleÃ§Ã£o)
147:   * -> GetItem direto Membership(userId, organizationId) -> TenantLifecycleRecord ACTIVE ->
148:   * RequestContext`. Wave B2B-6 (D-101) closes the transport gap D-095 left open (achado 2.1) -
149:   * `organizationIdHint` (from `X-Organization-Id`, BFF-derived, never client-controlled) is
150:   * revalidated via `resolveWorkingOrganization()` (consolidates Membership ACTIVE + lifecycle
151:   * ACTIVE - replaces the separate `AuthenticationError` this function used to throw for the
152:   * lifecycle half of that check). Hint absent falls back to deriving via GSI4: exactly one
153:   * ACTIVE Membership (today's common case, unchanged behavior) or a named, non-crashing error
154:   * for 0/>1 - no writer produced a 2nd ACTIVE Membership for the same user before Wave B2B-8
155:   * made it reachable, per D-095/D-096's own registered deviation.
156:   */
157:  private async resolveActiveMembership(userId: string, organizationIdHint: string | undefined): Promise<Membership> {
158:    if (organizationIdHint) {
159:      const result = await resolveWorkingOrganization(this.organizations, userId, organizationIdHint);
160:      if (result.status === "UNAVAILABLE") {
161:        throw new OrganizationUnavailableError("This organization is not available in your current context.", { organizationId: organizationIdHint });
162:      }
163:      return result.membership;
164:    }
165:
166:    const active = await resolveActiveMemberships(this.organizations, userId);
167:    if (active.length === 0) {
168:      throw new InternalError("OnboardingStateResolver reported HAS_USABLE_MEMBERSHIP but no ACTIVE Membership was found on re-resolution.", { userId });
169:    }
170:    if (active.length > 1) {
171:      throw new OrganizationSelectionRequiredError("Multiple organizations are available; select one via X-Organization-Id.", { userId, activeCount: active.length });
172:    }
173:    const [membership] = active;
174:    if (!membership) {
175:      throw new InternalError("Unreachable: active.length === 1 but active[0] is undefined.", { userId });
176:    }
177:    // Sem hint, a Ãºnica Membership ACTIVE ainda precisa da mesma checagem de lifecycle - nunca
178:    // um atalho que confie na hidrataÃ§Ã£o de resolveActiveMemberships() sozinha (essa funÃ§Ã£o sÃ³
179:    // checa Membership.status, nÃ£o TenantLifecycleRecord).
180:    const result = await resolveWorkingOrganization(this.organizations, userId, membership.organizationId);
181:    if (result.status === "UNAVAILABLE") {
182:      throw new OrganizationUnavailableError("This organization is not available in your current context.", { organizationId: membership.organizationId });
183:    }
184:    return result.membership;
185:  }
186:
187:  /** B2B-7 (D-097/D-098) closed the gap named in D-095: `authorization.ts` now recognizes all
188:   * 4 real `Membership.role` values. The assert stays (fail-closed, Codex Rodada 1 achado
189:   * 2.2/Rodada 2 mudanÃ§a F, D-095) for any value outside that domain â€” loud, not silent,
190:   * instead of letting an unrecognized role fail every `authorize()` check via that matrix's
191:   * own unsafe cast. */
192:  private resolveRoles(role: Membership["role"]): string[] {
193:    if (role !== "OWNER" && role !== "ADMIN" && role !== "MEMBER" && role !== "VIEWER") {
194:      throw new UnsupportedMembershipRoleError(role);
195:    }
196:    return [role];
197:  }
198:}
1:/**
2: * HTTP handler for W3-07's organization closure (D-124, implementing D-121) â€” the single real
3: * trigger surface for tenant deletion. Same pipeline and error-mapping convention as
4: * `membership-handlers.ts`/`organization-settings-handlers.ts` (resolve -> service, which
5: * internally calls `authorize()`), in its own file because closing the organization is a distinct
6: * concern from both membership management and settings.
7: *
8: * The typed confirmation lives in the request body, not just the UI. A destructive, irreversible
9: * action must not be triggerable by a bare POST that a mis-wired client (or a curious caller
10: * poking the API directly) could issue by accident â€” the client-side confirm dialog is a
11: * convenience, and the backend is the actual authority here, same posture as RBAC.
12: */
13:import { AppError, ValidationError, toAppError } from "../../../shared/errors/app-error.js";
14:import { AuthorizationDeniedError } from "../../identity/domain/authorization.js";
15:import { AuthorizationError } from "../../../shared/errors/app-error.js";
16:import { auditAuthorizationDenied } from "../../../shared/observability/security-audit.js";
17:import type { RequestContextResolver, ValidatedClaims } from "../../identity/application/resolve-request-context.js";
18:import type { CloseOrganizationService } from "../application/close-organization.js";
19:
20:export interface HttpRequest<TBody = unknown> {
21:  requestId: string;
22:  correlationId: string;
23:  claims: ValidatedClaims;
24:  headers?: Record<string, string | undefined>;
25:  body?: TBody;
26:}
27:
28:export interface HttpResponse {
29:  statusCode: number;
30:  body: Record<string, unknown>;
31:}
32:
33:export interface OrganizationLifecycleHttpDeps {
34:  resolver: RequestContextResolver;
35:  closeOrganization: CloseOrganizationService;
36:}
37:
38:export interface CloseOrganizationRequest {
39:  /** Must equal the organization's own id exactly â€” the type-to-confirm token. */
40:  confirmOrganizationId?: string;
41:}
42:
43:const STATUS_BY_CATEGORY: Record<string, number> = {
44:  VALIDATION: 400,
45:  AUTH: 401,
46:  AUTHORIZATION: 403,
47:  NOT_FOUND: 404,
48:  CONFLICT: 409,
49:  QUOTA_EXCEEDED: 429,
50:  DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE: 503,
51:  BUSINESS_RULE: 422,
52:  INTERNAL: 500,
53:};
54:
55:function toResponse(appError: AppError): HttpResponse {
56:  const status = STATUS_BY_CATEGORY[appError.category] ?? 500;
57:  return { statusCode: status, body: appError.toJSON() };
58:}
59:
60:async function withErrorMapping(fn: () => Promise<HttpResponse>): Promise<HttpResponse> {
61:  try {
62:    return await fn();
63:  } catch (err) {
64:    if (err instanceof AuthorizationDeniedError) {
65:      auditAuthorizationDenied({ reason: err.reason, action: err.action });
66:      return toResponse(new AuthorizationError(err.message, { reason: err.reason }));
67:    }
68:    const appError = err instanceof AppError ? err : toAppError(err);
69:    return toResponse(appError);
70:  }
71:}
72:
73:export async function handleCloseOrganization(deps: OrganizationLifecycleHttpDeps, req: HttpRequest<CloseOrganizationRequest>): Promise<HttpResponse> {
74:  return withErrorMapping(async () => {
75:    const context = await deps.resolver.resolve({ claims: req.claims, requestId: req.requestId, correlationId: req.correlationId, organizationIdHint: req.headers?.["x-organization-id"] });
76:
77:    // Compared against the RESOLVED tenantId, never against anything else the client sent - the
78:    // confirmation proves intent about the organization the caller is actually authenticated
79:    // into, so a stale browser tab pointed at a different org cannot close this one.
80:    if (req.body?.confirmOrganizationId !== context.tenant.tenantId) {
81:      throw new ValidationError("confirmOrganizationId must match the current organization id to confirm closure.");
82:    }
83:
84:    const result = await deps.closeOrganization.close(context);
85:    return { statusCode: 202, body: { organizationId: result.tenantId, status: result.status } };
86:  });
87:}

 succeeded in 428ms:
/**
 * TenantLifecycleRecord â€” W3-07 tenant deletion fence (D-067, `docs/architecture/decisions-log.md`;
 * design approved via Claude<->Codex protocol, 9.2/9.1, `docs/architecture/reviews/
 * w3-07-tenant-fence-round3-active-only-design/claude-analysis-active-only-fence.md` Â§G/Â§H).
 *
 * A tombstone item that outlives the tenant's own data cascade â€” same pattern as
 * `IdentityMapping` (identity-mapping-repository.ts): it lives outside the normal purge
 * cascade permanently, because it is the one artifact that must survive to prove "this
 * tenant existed and was deleted" and to block first-login from silently reprovisioning a
 * deleted tenant (the root cause the design doc traces D-063's failure to).
 *
 * Lives under `src/shared/` (not `src/modules/identity/domain/`) deliberately: it is
 * consumed by `src/shared/tenant-lifecycle/tenant-business-mutation.ts`, a shared/
 * infrastructure-level fencing primitive every tenant-scoped business mutation across
 * modules will eventually route through â€” and `shared/**` must never import from
 * `modules/**` (`.dependency-cruiser.cjs`'s `shared-must-not-reach-modules` rule). Placing
 * the record's own domain type here, instead of in `identity/domain` with the lane
 * importing it back out, keeps the dependency direction modules -> shared intact.
 */
import type { EntityKey } from "../dynamodb/occ.js";

/**
 * Forward-only state machine (approved design Â§G):
 *   ACTIVE -> DELETING -> QUIESCING -> PURGING -> VERIFIED -> DELETED
 * Any of DELETING/QUIESCING/PURGING/VERIFIED can additionally move to BLOCKED/HELD (stuck
 * state â€” legal hold or a purge error that keeps failing) and back to the SAME state once
 * remediated. ACTIVE is never re-entered from any other state (no "un-delete") and DELETED
 * is a true terminal â€” nothing transitions out of it (the sweeper described in the design
 * doc repairs residue in place, it never re-opens the lifecycle record's status).
 */
export type TenantLifecycleStatus =
  | "ACTIVE"
  | "DELETING"
  | "QUIESCING"
  | "PURGING"
  | "VERIFIED"
  | "DELETED"
  | "BLOCKED"
  | "HELD";

export interface TenantLifecycleRecord extends EntityKey {
  SK: "LIFECYCLE";
  entityType: "TenantLifecycleRecord";
  tenantId: string;
  status: TenantLifecycleStatus;
  /** Set when entering BLOCKED/HELD and cleared on remediation back to the prior state â€” a
   * short machine-readable reason for the stuck state (e.g. "PURGE_S3_ERRORS",
   * "LEGAL_HOLD"), never free text containing tenant PII. */
  blockedReason?: string;
  /** The state BLOCKED/HELD was entered from, so remediation knows where to resume â€”
   * required whenever status is BLOCKED/HELD, absent otherwise. */
  blockedFrom?: TenantLifecycleStatus;
  createdAt: string;
  updatedAt: string;
  version: number;
}

export function tenantLifecycleKey(tenantId: string): EntityKey {
  return { PK: `TENANT#${tenantId}#LIFECYCLE`, SK: "LIFECYCLE" };
}

/**
 * Statuses under which a `TenantBusinessMutation` may be admitted â€” deliberately just
 * `ACTIVE` (approved design Â§H invariant 1: "Nenhuma mutaÃ§Ã£o de negÃ³cio tenant-scoped
 * commita em DynamoDB sem ConditionCheck de TenantLifecycleRecord.status = ACTIVE").
 */
export const TENANT_ACTIVE_STATUS: TenantLifecycleStatus = "ACTIVE";

/** Forward-only transition graph. A state maps to the set of states it may legally move to
 * next; BLOCKED/HELD's own allowed targets depend on `blockedFrom` (checked separately by
 * `canResumeFromBlocked`, since the graph itself can't express "return to wherever you came
 * from" as a fixed edge list). */
const FORWARD_TRANSITIONS: Record<TenantLifecycleStatus, ReadonlySet<TenantLifecycleStatus>> = {
  ACTIVE: new Set(["DELETING"]),
  DELETING: new Set(["QUIESCING", "BLOCKED", "HELD"]),
  QUIESCING: new Set(["PURGING", "BLOCKED", "HELD"]),
  PURGING: new Set(["VERIFIED", "BLOCKED", "HELD"]),
  VERIFIED: new Set(["DELETED", "BLOCKED", "HELD"]),
  DELETED: new Set([]),
  BLOCKED: new Set([]), // only canResumeFromBlocked() below, not a fixed forward edge
  HELD: new Set([]),
};

/**
 * True if `from -> to` is a legal forward transition, OR a remediation resume out of
 * BLOCKED/HELD back to the exact state it was blocked from. Never true for anything that
 * would re-enter ACTIVE or leave DELETED â€” those two guarantees are the entire point of
 * this fence (approved design Â§H invariant 4, Â§Q "no ACTIVE reversion, no DELETED
 * resurrection").
 */
export function canTransition(from: TenantLifecycleStatus, to: TenantLifecycleStatus, blockedFrom?: TenantLifecycleStatus): boolean {
  if (to === "ACTIVE") return false; // never re-enter ACTIVE from anywhere
  if (from === "DELETED") return false; // DELETED is terminal, nothing leaves it
  if ((from === "BLOCKED" || from === "HELD") && blockedFrom) {
    return to === blockedFrom;
  }
  return FORWARD_TRANSITIONS[from]?.has(to) ?? false;
}

export class InvalidTenantLifecycleTransitionError extends Error {
  constructor(
    public readonly from: TenantLifecycleStatus,
    public readonly to: TenantLifecycleStatus,
  ) {
    super(`Invalid TenantLifecycleRecord transition: ${from} -> ${to}`);
    this.name = "InvalidTenantLifecycleTransitionError";
  }
}

/** Throws InvalidTenantLifecycleTransitionError instead of returning false â€” for callers
 * (e.g. the future lifecycle-transition worker) that want a fail-fast assertion rather than
 * a boolean they must remember to check. */
export function assertValidTransition(from: TenantLifecycleStatus, to: TenantLifecycleStatus, blockedFrom?: TenantLifecycleStatus): void {
  if (!canTransition(from, to, blockedFrom)) {
    throw new InvalidTenantLifecycleTransitionError(from, to);
  }
}


 succeeded in 463ms:
/**
 * CloseOrganizationService â€” W3-07 purge orchestrator (D-124, implementing D-121's approved
 * design, Rodada 2 Fix 1 with Rodada 3 Fix 8's corrected step ordering).
 *
 * The single real trigger surface for tenant deletion: moves the tenant's `TenantLifecycleRecord`
 * `ACTIVE -> DELETING` and launches the tenant-purge Step Functions execution that drives every
 * remaining transition. Before this service existed, both `transitionTenantLifecycle` and
 * `purgeTenant()` were real, working, and completely unreachable â€” nothing in production ever
 * called either.
 *
 * Step ordering is load-bearing and was corrected during the design protocol (Rodada 3 Fix 8): the
 * terminal-state check runs BEFORE the unconditional `StartExecution`, not after. Rodada 2's
 * original wording implied any post-`ACTIVE` status should trigger a fresh launch, which would
 * have re-launched executions for VERIFIED/DELETED/BLOCKED/HELD tenants â€” states where a purge
 * execution is either finished or deliberately parked awaiting an operator.
 *
 * `StartExecution` IS called unconditionally for the genuinely in-flight states
 * (`DELETING`/`QUIESCING`/`PURGING`), including when this call performed no write at all. That is
 * the established `start-extraction-run.ts` idiom, not an oversight: gating the launch on "did I
 * just write the record" orphans the tenant forever whenever the DynamoDB transition commits but
 * the subsequent `StartExecution` fails transiently â€” the record would sit in DELETING with no
 * execution behind it, and every retry would skip the launch because the write already happened.
 * Step Functions' own name-based uniqueness (`name: tenantId`) is what makes calling it every
 * time safe.
 */
import { authorize } from "../../identity/domain/authorization.js";
import type { RequestContext } from "../../identity/domain/request-context.js";
import { OrganizationClosureUnavailableError, NotFoundError } from "../../../shared/errors/app-error.js";
import { SystemMutationConflictError, transitionTenantLifecycle, type SystemMutationStore } from "../../../shared/tenant-lifecycle/system-mutation.js";
import type { TenantLifecycleRecord, TenantLifecycleStatus } from "../../../shared/tenant-lifecycle/tenant-lifecycle-record.js";
import type { TenantPurgeExecutionStarter } from "../../../shared/tenant-lifecycle/tenant-purge-execution-starter.js";

/** Same narrow read contract the orchestrator's transition handler uses â€” `ConsistentRead: true`
 * is mandatory, since this read produces the `expectedVersion` the OCC-fenced transition below is
 * conditioned on. */
export interface TenantLifecycleReader {
  read(tenantId: string): Promise<TenantLifecycleRecord | undefined>;
}

/** States where a purge is either already finished or parked awaiting a human â€” closing is
 * refused outright rather than re-launched. Named "unavailable", not "terminal": an operator can
 * still remediate BLOCKED/HELD (`tenant-lifecycle-record.ts`), so these are not all true terminal
 * states. */
const CLOSURE_UNAVAILABLE_STATUSES: ReadonlySet<TenantLifecycleStatus> = new Set(["VERIFIED", "DELETED", "BLOCKED", "HELD"]);

/** The genuinely in-flight states â€” a close() call landing here performs no write and only
 * repeats the idempotent launch. */
const IN_FLIGHT_STATUSES: ReadonlySet<TenantLifecycleStatus> = new Set(["DELETING", "QUIESCING", "PURGING"]);

export interface CloseOrganizationResult {
  tenantId: string;
  status: TenantLifecycleStatus;
  /** False when the record was already DELETING+ and this call only repeated the launch. */
  transitioned: boolean;
}

export class CloseOrganizationService {
  constructor(
    private readonly store: SystemMutationStore,
    private readonly reader: TenantLifecycleReader,
    private readonly executions: TenantPurgeExecutionStarter,
    private readonly tableName: string,
    private readonly now: () => string = () => new Date().toISOString(),
  ) {}

  async close(ctx: RequestContext): Promise<CloseOrganizationResult> {
    const tenantId = ctx.tenant.tenantId;
    authorize({ context: ctx, action: "organization:close", resource: { tenantId } });

    const record = await this.reader.read(tenantId);
    if (!record) {
      throw new NotFoundError("Organization lifecycle record not found.", { organizationId: tenantId });
    }
    if (CLOSURE_UNAVAILABLE_STATUSES.has(record.status)) {
      throw new OrganizationClosureUnavailableError(undefined, { organizationId: tenantId, status: record.status });
    }

    let transitioned = false;
    if (record.status === "ACTIVE") {
      try {
        await transitionTenantLifecycle({
          store: this.store,
          tableName: this.tableName,
          tenantId,
          from: "ACTIVE",
          to: "DELETING",
          expectedVersion: record.version,
          now: this.now,
        });
        transitioned = true;
      } catch (err) {
        if (!(err instanceof SystemMutationConflictError)) throw err;
        // A concurrent close() (double-click, retried request) won the race. Re-read once: if the
        // record legitimately landed on an in-flight state, this call simply joins that closure
        // and proceeds to the shared idempotent launch below. Anything else â€” still ACTIVE, or
        // raced into a stuck state â€” is genuinely unexpected and is not papered over.
        const after = await this.reader.read(tenantId);
        if (!after || !IN_FLIGHT_STATUSES.has(after.status)) throw err;
      }
    } else if (!IN_FLIGHT_STATUSES.has(record.status)) {
      // Exhaustiveness guard: ACTIVE, the 4 unavailable states, and the 3 in-flight states cover
      // every TenantLifecycleStatus. Reaching here means a status was added without deciding what
      // closing should do with it â€” fail loudly rather than silently launching an execution.
      throw new OrganizationClosureUnavailableError(undefined, { organizationId: tenantId, status: record.status });
    }

    await this.executions.startExecution({ name: tenantId, input: { tenantId } });

    return { tenantId, status: transitioned ? "DELETING" : record.status, transitioned };
  }
}
